│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │

│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Explorer agent of the Multi Agent System.               │
│  • Based on the provided task by the user, use the given tools to gather     │
│    some basic information relevant to completing the task.                   │
│  • Do not try to solve the tasks, instead, collect only basic information    │
│    that will assist later agents in planning and actively solving the task.  │
│  • When finished write a concise summary of only the gathered information    │
│    and include the tag '[solver]'.                                           │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                         Code Execution Capabilities                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your are equipped with different tools for executing code.                │
│  • Do only use the tools to execute code. Code inside a Markdown Code Block  │
│    will not be executed.                                                     │
│  • Use Shell tool for simple, single bash commands. Do not use it for        │
│    commands that open interactive terminal programs (e.g. git log or man).   │
│  • Use Terminal tool to execute commands in a persistent terminal instance   │
│    and get back the exact terminal screen. This is especially useful for     │
│    interactive programs such as git log, man, or vim.                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                             More Operating Rules                             │
│                                                                              │

│  • Break complex objectives into smaller sub-goals if necessary.             │
│  • Use tools to gather knowledge or verify assumptions before proceeding.    │
│  • If you call a tool it will be executed and results will be returned to    │
│    you.                                                                      │

│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Tester agent of the Multi Agent System.                 │
│  • The given task is already solved by the previous agents.                  │
│  • Your task is now to test if the overall user's task was completed         │
│    successfully.                                                             │
│  • Use the information provided by previous agents, along with your          │
│    available tools, to test the solution.                                    │
│  • If you come to the conclusion that the task is not solved successfully    │
│    and entirely, describe concisely why it is not solved and include the tag │
│    '[solver]'.                                                               │
│  • If the task is solved successfully write a summary of what you did and    │
│    include the tag '[approver]'.                                             │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │

╭──────────────────── logging - approver - system_message ─────────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your role is the "Approver" agent of the Multi Agent System.              │
│  • Your job is to determine if the agents have done enough work on the task. │
│  • Make your decision based on all information gathered and the actions      │
│    performed by previous agents.                                             │
│  • Be critical and selective, approve only what you are completely sure of.  │
│  • If you decide that the task was not solved successfully, briefly explain  │
│    why and include the tag '[solver]'.                                       │
│  • If you decide that not enough testing was done to ensure the task is      │
│    fully and successfully completed, briefly explain why and add the tag     │
│    '[tester]'.                                                               │
│  • If you decide that sufficient work was done, just reply briefly with the  │
│    tag '[summarizer]'.                                                       │
│  • Make one of the three decisions and include its tag in your response.     │

│ <output> # Notes On Compiling SQLite On All Kinds Of Unix                    │
│                                                                              │
│ Here are step-by-step instructions on how to build SQLite from canonical     │
│ source on any modern machine that isn't Windows.  These notes are tested (on │
│ 2024-10-11) on Ubuntu and on MacOS, but they are general and should work on  │
│ most any modern unix platform. See the companion document () for guidance on │
│ building for Windows.                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  1 Install a C-compiler.  GCC or Clang both work fine.  If you are reading   │
│    this document, you've probably already done that.                         │
│  2 (Optional): Install TCL development libraries.  In this note, we'll do a  │
│    private install in the $HOME/local directory, but you can make            │
│    adjustments to install TCL wherever you like. This document assumes you   │
│    are working with TCL version 9.0. See also the  document that contains    │
│    more details on compiling Tcl for use with SQLite. <ol type="a"> <li>Get  │
│    the TCL source archive, perhaps from                                      │
│    <https://www.tcl.tk/software/tcltk/download.html> or                      │
│    <https://sqlite.org/tmp/tcl9.0.0.tar.gz>. <li>Untar the source archive.   │
│    CD into the "unix/" subfolder of the source tree. <li>Run: mkdir          │
│    $HOME/local <li>Run: ./configure --prefix=$HOME/local <li>Run: make       │
│    install </ol> <p> As of 2024-10-25, TCL is not longer required for many   │
│    common build targets, such as "sqlite3.c" or the "sqlite3" command-line   │
│    tool.  So you can skip this step if that is all you want to build.  TCL   │
│    is still required to run "make test" and similar, or to build the TCL     │
│    extension, of course.                                                     │
│  3 Download the SQLite source tree and unpack it. CD into the toplevel       │
│    directory of the source tree.                                             │
│  4 Run: ./configure --enable-all --with-tclsh=$HOME/local/bin/tclsh9.0       │
│    You do not need to use --with-tclsh if the tclsh you want to use is the   │
│    first one on your PATH or if you are building without TCL.                │
│  5 Run the "Makefile" makefile with an appropriate target. Examples: <ul>    │
│    <li>  make sqlite3.c <li>  make sqlite3 <li>  make sqldiff <li>  make     │
│    sqlite3_rsync </ul> <p>None of the targets above require TCL.  TCL is     │
│    needed for the following targets: <ul> <li>  make tclextension-install    │
│    <li>  make devtest <li>  make releasetest <li>  make sqlite3_analyzer     │
│    </ul>                                                                     │
│    It is not required that you run the "tclextension-install" target prior   │
│    to running tests.  However, the tests will run more smoothly if you do.   │
│    The version of SQLite used for the TCL extension does not need to         │
│    correspond to the version of SQLite under test.  So you can install the   │
│    SQLite TCL extension once, and then use it to test many different         │
│    versions of SQLite.                                                       │
│  6 For a debugging build of the CLI, where the ".treetrace" and              │
│    ".wheretrace" commands work, add the the --with-debug argument to         │
│    configure. </output>                                                      │

│ ┃     $(CFLAGS) and $(CPPFLAGS) here because that's the way the legacy     ┃ │

│ ┃     build did it and many builds rely on that. See main.mk for more      ┃ │

│ ┃   configure-time, and expanded them into the generated Makefile. There   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃    are, in that build, no uses of CPPFLAGS in the configure-expanded     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃    Makefile. Ergo: if a client configures with CPPFLAGS=... and then     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃        explicitly passes CFLAGS=... to make, the CPPFLAGS will be        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                lost. That behavior is retained in 3.48+.                 ┃ │

│ proc sqlite-configure {buildMode configScript} { proj-assert                 │
│ {$::sqliteConfig(build-mode) eq "unknown"}                                   │
│ "sqlite-configure must not be called more than once" set allBuildModes       │
│ {canonical autoconf} if {$buildMode ni $allBuildModes} { user-error "Invalid │
│ build mode: $buildMode. Expecting one of: $allBuildModes" } if               │
│ {$::sqliteConfig(all-flag-default)} { set allFlagHelp "Disable these         │
│ extensions: $::sqliteConfig(all-flag-enables)" } else { set allFlagHelp      │
│ "Enable these extensions: $::sqliteConfig(all-flag-enables)" }               │
│                                                                              │
│ set ::sqliteConfig(build-mode) $buildMode                                    │

│ ┃      stringopt:         => "an option which takes an argument, e.g.      ┃ │

│ ┃        especially booleans. Each bool-type --FLAG implicitly gets        ┃ │

│ ┃    --enable-FLAG and --disable-FLAG forms. That can lead lead to some    ┃ │

│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   The reason the help text says "disable" is because a boolean option    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃       which defaults to true is, in the --help text, rendered as:        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃              --disable-json          Disable JSON functions              ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   Whereas a bool flag which defaults to false will instead render as:    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                              --enable-FLAG                               ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   Non-boolean flags, in contrast, use the names specifically given to    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   them in the [options] invocation. e.g. "with-tcl" is the --with-tcl    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                  flag.                                   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                        Fetching values for flags:                        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                          booleans: use one of:                           ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    - [opt-bool FLAG] is autosetup's built-in command for this, but we    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                     have some convenience variants:                      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                         - [proj-opt-truthy FLAG]                         ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                - [proj-opt-if-truthy FLAG {THEN} {ELSE}]                 ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                     Non-boolean (i.e. string) flags:                     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃         - [opt-str ...] - see the docs in ./autosetup/autosetup          ┃ │

│ ┃   [proj-opt-was-provided] can be used to determine whether a flag was    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    explicitly provided, which is often useful for distinguishing from    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃                       the case of a default value.                       ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ######################################################################## set │
│ allFlags { # Structure: a list of M {Z} pairs, where M is a descriptive #    │
│ option group name  and Z is a list of X Y pairs. X is a list of # $buildMode │
│ name(s) to which the Y flags apply, or {*} to apply # to all builds. Y is a  │
│ {block} in the form expected by # autosetup's [options] command.  Each block │
│ which is applicable # to $buildMode is appended to a new list before that    │
│ list is # passed on to [options]. The order of each Y and sub-Y is #         │
│ retained, which is significant for rendering of --help.                      │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  # When writing {help text blocks}, be aware that:                           │
│  #                                                                           │
│  # A) autosetup formats them differently if the {block} starts with          │
│  # a newline: it starts left-aligned, directly under the --flag, and         │
│  # the rest of the block is pasted verbatim rather than                      │
│  # pretty-printed.                                                           │
│  #                                                                           │
│  # B) Vars and commands are NOT expanded, but we use a [subst] call          │
│  # below which will replace (only) var refs.                                 │
│                                                                              │
│  # Options for how to build the library                                      │
│  build-modes {                                                               │
│    {canonical autoconf} {                                                    │
│      shared=1             =&gt; {Disable build of shared library}            │
│      static=1             =&gt; {Disable build of static library}            │
│    }                                                                         │
│    {canonical} {                                                             │
│      amalgamation=1       =&gt; {Disable the amalgamation and instead build  │
│  all files separately}                                                       │
│    }                                                                         │
│  }                                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  # Library-level features and defaults                                       │
│  lib-features {                                                              │
│    {*} {                                                                     │
│      threadsafe=1         =&gt; {Disable mutexing}                           │
│      with-tempstore:=no   =&gt; {Use an in-RAM database for temporary        │
│  tables: never,no,yes,always}                                                │
│      load-extension=1     =&gt; {Disable loading of external extensions}     │
│      # ^^^ one of the downstream custom builds overrides the load-extension  │
│  default to 0, which                                                         │
│      # confuses the --help text generator.                                   │
│  https://github.com/msteveb/autosetup/issues/77                              │
│      math=1               =&gt; {Disable math functions}                     │
│      json=1               =&gt; {Disable JSON functions}                     │
│      memsys5              =&gt; {Enable MEMSYS5}                             │
│      memsys3              =&gt; {Enable MEMSYS3}                             │
│      fts3                 =&gt; {Enable the FTS3 extension}                  │
│      fts4                 =&gt; {Enable the FTS4 extension}                  │
│      fts5                 =&gt; {Enable the FTS5 extension}                  │
│      update-limit         =&gt; {Enable the UPDATE/DELETE LIMIT clause}      │
│      geopoly              =&gt; {Enable the GEOPOLY extension}               │
│      rtree                =&gt; {Enable the RTREE extension}                 │
│      session              =&gt; {Enable the SESSION extension}               │
│      all=$::sqliteConfig(all-flag-default) =&gt; {$allFlagHelp}              │
│      largefile=1                                                             │
│        =&gt; {This legacy flag has no effect on the library but may          │
│  influence                                                                   │
│            the generated sqlite_cfg.h by adding #define HAVE_LFS}            │
│    }                                                                         │
│  }                                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  # Options for TCL support                                                   │
│  tcl {                                                                       │
│    {canonical} {                                                             │
│      tcl=1                                                                   │
│        =&gt; {Disable components which require TCL, including all tests.     │
│            This tree requires TCL for code generation but can use the        │
│  in-tree                                                                     │
│            copy of autosetup/jimsh0.c for that. The SQLite TCL extension     │
│  and the                                                                     │
│            test code require a canonical tclsh.}                             │
│    }                                                                         │
│    {canonical} {                                                             │
│      with-tcl:DIR                                                            │
│        =&gt; {Directory containing tclConfig.sh or a directory one level up  │
│  from                                                                        │
│            that, from which we can derive a directory containing             │
│  tclConfig.sh.                                                               │
│            A dir name of &quot;prefix&quot; is equivalent to the directory   │
│  specified by                                                                │
│            the --prefix flag.}                                               │
│      with-tclsh:PATH                                                         │
│        =&gt; {Full pathname of tclsh to use.  It is used for (A) trying to   │
│  find                                                                        │
│            tclConfig.sh and (B) all TCL-based code generation.  Warning: if  │
│            its containing dir has multiple tclsh versions, it may select     │
│  the                                                                         │
│            wrong tclConfig.sh!}                                              │
│    }                                                                         │
│    {canonical} {                                                             │
│      static-tclsqlite3=0                                                     │
│        =&gt; {Statically-link tclsqlite3. This only works if TCL support is  │
│            enabled and all requisite libraries are available in              │
│            static form. Note that glibc is unable to fully statically        │
│            link certain libraries required by tclsqlite3, so this            │
│  won&#x27;t                                                                  │
│            work on most Linux environments.}                                 │
│    }                                                                         │
│  }                                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  # Options for line-editing modes for the CLI shell                          │
│  line-editing {                                                              │
│    {canonical autoconf} {                                                    │
│      readline=1                                                              │
│        =&gt; {Disable readline support}                                      │
│      # --with-readline-lib is a backwards-compatible alias for               │
│      # --with-readline-ldflags                                               │
│      with-readline-lib:                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ </output>                                                                    │

│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃        JimTCL, a copy of which is included in this source tree as        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃      ./autosetup/jimsh0.c. The number of incompatibilities between       ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   canonical TCL and JimTCL is very low and alternative formulations of   ┃ │

│     • Enabling coverage via the provided build system is done by running     │

│ -ftest-coverage and linked with -lgcov (visible in configure/build           │
│ output).\n- Instrumentation artifacts present: .gcno files in the build      │
│ tree, and .gcda files produced after running the instrumented binary.\n-     │
│ sqlite3 is installed and on PATH: which sqlite3 -> /usr/local/bin/sqlite3;   │
│ sqlite3 --version -> 3.50.4 (...) — gcc/gcov/make toolchain was present and  │
│ used.\n- Coverage notes: running the instrumented sqlite3 generates .gcda    │
│ files in the directories where the instrumented objects live; gcov/lcov +    │
│ genhtml can be used to produce human-readable coverage reports.\n\nKey       │
│ findings / lessons\n- The project's configure already supports a --gcov      │
│ option that correctly toggles USE_GCOV and injects the right compile/link    │
│ flags, so no manual Makefile edits were needed.\n- System dev libraries      │
│ (readline, zlib) can affect linking depending on configure options; they     │
│ were not an obstacle here but may be required in other environments.\n-      │
│ Coverage data collection and reporting require exercising the built binary   │
│ and then running gcov/lcov on the resulting .gcno/.gcda files.\n\nIf you     │
│ want, I can run a small workload to generate more .gcda files and            │
